28: ## 1. Complete fresh fixed-sum row bound, arbitrary values including zero
29: 
30: For an ACTUAL multiset S of N occurrences in F_p, let v_x(S) be its multiplicities and Sigma_n(S) the sums of exactly n DISTINCT OCCURRENCES, 1<=n<=N. Put
31: 
32:     T_n(S)=sum_x min(n,v_x(S)).
33: 
34: **Lemma1.1 (rows).**
35: 
36:     |Sigma_n(S)|>=min(p,T_n(S)-n+1).                      (1)
37: 
38: **Proof.** For each value x keep exactly min(n,v_x) of its actual occurrences; list these T=T_n occurrences VALUE BY VALUE. Number them k=0,...,T-1 and place occurrence k in row(k modn)+1. One value's kept occurrences are consecutive and number at most n, so no row contains that value twice. Thus each row A_i is a SET of values, with its actual occurrence identified, and rows have disjoint occurrence sets.
39: 
40: T>=n: if some v_x>=n its contribution already givesn; otherwise T=N>=n. Cyclic distribution therefore fills all n rows at least once. All A_i are NONEMPTY. Choosing one occurrence in EACH row gives an actual n-occurrence selection in S, so
41: 
42:     A_1+...+A_n subset Sigma_n(S).
43: 
44: Iterate classical Cauchy-Davenport on the NONEMPTY rowsets (all intermediate sumsets are nonempty). It gives
45: 
46:     |A_1+...+A_n|>=min(p,sum_i|A_i|-n+1)
47:                    =min(p,T_n(S)-n+1).
48: 
49: This proves(1). Actual value0 is allowed; no dummy zero or nonzero-term assumption from RS(b) is used. QED.
50: 
51: **Corollary1.2.** If ANY residue is absent from Sigma_n(S), then
52: 
53:     T_n(S)<=p+n-2.                                      (2)
54:

58: 
59:     Sigma_(N-n)(S)=sigma(S)-Sigma_n(S).                  (3)
60: 
61: Hence fullness of either fixed-size sumset gives fullness of the complementary size. No zero-sum length, odd lift, kappa range or word normal form is assumed in(1)–(3).

63: ## 2. SR2: an all-count signed-row TWO-reflection word criterion

Assign each actual rotation rho_x sign+ beforei/afterj and sign- strictly betweeni,j. Let X^{i,j} be this SIGNED rotation multiset, of lengthR. Unselected reflections do not flip signs.

141: 
142: If A>=1, start at any cyclic gap and follow integer prefix sums0,...,D. The final D>=2A+p-1 exceeds A, so a first prefix reaching A exists. If their parity indicators are alpha,beta in{0,1}, the merged parity is alpha XORbeta, so the change is

All counts are of the ACTUAL new decomposition, not assumed inherited parameters. QED.

38: ## 2. Exact group algebra, ideal and low-degree moments — fully re-proved
39: 
40: Let A=F_p[H] have basis E_(v,h), multiplication E_(v,h)E_(w,k)=E_(v+w,h+k), identityE_(0,0). It is commutative, dimensionp^2. X=E_(1,0),Y=E_(0,1) satisfy X^p=Y^p=1. Put s=X-1,t=Y-1. In characteristicp,
41: 
42:     s^p=X^p-1=0,t^p=0.
43: 
44: The map F_p[s,t]/(s^p,t^p)->A is surjective since X=1+s,Y=1+t generate every group-basis point. Both spaces have dimensionp^2 (monomials s^i t^j,0<=i,j<p in the quotient), hence the map is an isomorphism.
45: 
46: Let J be the AUGMENTATION IDEAL, i.e. the kernel of sum-of-coefficients. It is(s,t). Any monomial of totaldegree>=2p-1 has s-exponent>=p or t-exponent>=p and is zero. Thus
47: 
48:     J^(2p-1)=0.                                        (3)
49: 
50: This is an IDEAL in an explicitly truncated COMMUTATIVE group algebra, not a matrix J(a) or evaluation at nonzero a for s. Forbidden parent matrix/dictionary/eigenvalue outputs are irrelevant and inadmissible; no such output is used here.
51: 
52: For residuesv,h, taking representatives0..p-1,
53: 
54:     E_(v,h)=(1+s)^v(1+t)^h.
55: 
56: Its coefficient of s^i t^j for i+j<=2 is binom(v,i)binom(h,j). Those are well-defined polynomials in F_p because i!,j! are nonzero at these degrees. Therefore, for d=1,2,3,
57: 
58:     theta inJ^d iff sum_S lambda_S P(phi(S))=0
59:     for EVERY polynomialP(v,h) of totaldegree<d.         (4)
60: 
61: Indeed expansion makes the binomial moments of totaldegree<d exactly the quotient coefficients that must vanish. For degrees<=2 these binomial polynomials span the ordinary monomials1,v,h,v^2,vh,h^2, since2is invertible. Conversely those moments kill every lower-degree coefficient. We make NO analogous ordinary-moment assertion at degrees>=p; it is not needed. Multiplication of ideal powers handles all larger global depths.
62: 
63: A unit has depthd if its actual option element lies inJ^d. All constructions below have depth EXACTLY AT LEAST half their block length; equality of the lower bound, not a maximum-depth assertion, suffices globally.

130: 
131: This supplies actual coefficients, not an unproved existence/solver claim. Suppose a six-letter actual unit has admissible nonempty option points containing

140:     M_j(v)=(v-y_(3-j))/(y_j-y_(3-j)), j1,2.

143: 
144:     B_j=-M_j(0)-2M_j(z),
145:     C_i=L_i(0)-L_i(z)-sum_(j=1,2) B_j L_i(y_j).          (7)

161:

163: 
164:     Q(0,0)=P_2(0)-P_1(0)+2c,
165:     Q(z,3)=P_2(z)+2P_1(z)+2c.
166:

174: 
175: and quadratic interpolation gives P_2(v)=sum_i L_i(v)Q(x_i,1). Substitute both: the resulting coefficients are EXACTLY(7), proving(9). All interpolations are polynomials of the stated degree over F_p, not samples over selected primes.

Point levels1,2,3 are nonzero inF_p forp>=7, so each chosen representative is genuinely nonempty; the distinguished empty choice has coefficient1. All selections have the actual prescribed parity. QED.
